# Chapter 19 — Domain-Driven Application Architecture

# Part I

---

# 19.1 Introduction

Modern Clinical Decision Support Systems (CDSS) extend far beyond conventional web applications. They integrate complex clinical workflows, structured domain knowledge, symbolic reasoning, semantic models, governance mechanisms, and explainable decision support. Consequently, their architecture must faithfully represent the problem domain rather than the underlying implementation technology.

Although Django provides an effective framework for developing secure web applications, its default Model–View–Template (MVT) architecture encourages the co-location of persistence logic, business rules, and presentation concerns. Such an approach is appropriate for many business applications but becomes increasingly difficult to maintain as the complexity of a knowledge-centric clinical platform grows.

NutriLogic is fundamentally different from a traditional CRUD-based web application. It is a **knowledge-centric neuro-symbolic Clinical Nutrition Decision Support System (CNDSS)** whose primary purpose is to acquire, represent, reason over, explain, and evolve nutrition knowledge. Clinical reasoning is delegated to a symbolic inference engine implemented in SWI-Prolog, while Django serves as the orchestration platform responsible for workflow management, security, persistence, interoperability, and user interaction.

To preserve this separation of concerns, the backend is designed using **Domain-Driven Design (DDD)** in conjunction with **Hexagonal Architecture (Ports and Adapters)**. Rather than organizing software around technical layers such as models, views, and serializers, the application is organized around clinical capabilities that correspond to real-world nutrition practice.

Each business capability forms an independent **Bounded Context**, encapsulating its own domain model, services, repositories, events, and interfaces. These contexts collaborate through explicitly defined contracts while remaining independently maintainable and testable.

This architectural approach provides several important benefits.

First, it isolates domain knowledge from framework-specific implementation details, allowing clinical logic to evolve independently of Django.

Second, it enables the symbolic reasoning engine to remain replaceable without affecting the remainder of the application.

Third, it promotes high cohesion within each clinical capability while minimizing coupling between unrelated domains.

Finally, it establishes a robust architectural foundation for future neuro-symbolic extensions in which machine learning components augment—but do not replace—the deterministic symbolic reasoning process.

This chapter realizes the **Development View**, **Logical View**, and portions of the **Knowledge View** established in Chapter 18.

---

# 19.2 Objectives

The Domain-Driven Application Architecture seeks to establish a maintainable, extensible, and clinically meaningful software structure for NutriLogic.

Its objectives are to:

* Organize the application around clinical business capabilities rather than technical frameworks.
* Separate clinical knowledge from infrastructure concerns.
* Protect domain logic from framework dependencies.
* Support independent evolution of bounded contexts.
* Facilitate integration with the symbolic reasoning engine through well-defined interfaces.
* Promote maintainability through high cohesion and loose coupling.
* Improve testability by isolating business logic from persistence and presentation technologies.
* Enable future neuro-symbolic extensions without disrupting the deterministic reasoning core.
* Support governance, explainability, interoperability, and knowledge evolution as first-class architectural concerns.

These objectives directly support the architectural principles established in Chapter 2 and realize the integration strategy defined in Chapter 17.

---

# 19.3 Architectural Context

Within the overall NutriLogic platform, the Domain-Driven Application Architecture occupies the central coordination layer between user-facing interfaces and the underlying knowledge-centric infrastructure.

```text
                    React Frontend
                           │
                           ▼
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
          Domain-Driven Application Architecture
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 Users Context
 Assessment Context
 Knowledge Context
 Inference Context
 Intervention Context
 Explanation Context
 Monitoring Context
 Analytics Context
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
                           │
        ┌──────────────────┴──────────────────┐
        ▼                                     ▼
 Knowledge Infrastructure              Platform Infrastructure
 (Ontology, KG, Prolog)               (PostgreSQL, Redis, Files)
```

Unlike conventional web applications, the application layer **does not perform clinical reasoning**.

Its responsibilities include:

* coordinating workflows,
* validating inputs,
* managing transactions,
* enforcing governance,
* orchestrating communication,
* maintaining auditability,
* coordinating inference requests.

Clinical reasoning remains exclusively within the symbolic reasoning subsystem described in Chapters 11–15.

Consequently, the application architecture functions as an orchestrator rather than a decision-maker.

---

# 19.4 Domain-Driven Design Overview

Domain-Driven Design (DDD) is an architectural methodology that emphasizes modelling software around the problem domain instead of technical implementation details.

Within NutriLogic, the "domain" is **clinical nutrition care**, encompassing patient assessment, nutrition diagnosis, intervention planning, monitoring, knowledge management, and explainable clinical reasoning.

DDD distinguishes between **strategic design** and **tactical design**.

## Strategic Design

Strategic design identifies the major business domains and establishes explicit boundaries between them.

In NutriLogic these boundaries are represented as **Bounded Contexts**, each responsible for a distinct clinical capability.

Examples include:

* Assessment
* Knowledge
* Inference
* Intervention
* Monitoring
* Explainability
* Analytics

Each bounded context owns its own:

* terminology,
* entities,
* value objects,
* repositories,
* domain services,
* events,
* interfaces.

No context directly manipulates another context's internal models.

Communication occurs through explicit contracts.

## Tactical Design

Within each bounded context, DDD employs several modelling constructs:

* Entities
* Value Objects
* Aggregate Roots
* Domain Services
* Repositories
* Domain Events
* Application Services

These constructs collectively model the behaviour, state, and lifecycle of clinical concepts while preserving consistency and encapsulation.

---

# 19.5 Architectural Design Principles

The Domain-Driven Application Architecture is governed by the following principles.

## Principle 1 — Domain-Centric Organization

Software structure shall reflect clinical nutrition practice rather than implementation technologies.

Business capabilities define software boundaries.

---

## Principle 2 — Persistence Ignorance

Domain entities shall remain independent of database technologies.

Clinical concepts should exist independently of Django ORM models.

---

## Principle 3 — Framework Independence

The application core shall not depend upon Django, PostgreSQL, PySWIP, or any external framework.

Frameworks become replaceable infrastructure components.

---

## Principle 4 — Explicit Bounded Contexts

Each clinical capability shall define an explicit boundary with clearly owned responsibilities.

Cross-context dependencies shall be minimized.

---

## Principle 5 — Rich Domain Models

Business rules shall reside within domain entities and domain services rather than presentation or persistence layers.

---

## Principle 6 — Hexagonal Architecture

All external technologies communicate with the application core through Ports and Adapters.

The application depends on abstractions rather than implementations.

---

## Principle 7 — Knowledge Isolation

Knowledge representation, ontology management, and symbolic reasoning remain separate from application orchestration.

This protects clinical knowledge from application-specific concerns.

---

## Principle 8 — Explainability as a Core Capability

Recommendation explanations constitute an independent architectural capability.

Explainability is therefore modelled as a dedicated domain rather than an auxiliary feature.

---

## Principle 9 — Event-Driven Collaboration

Bounded contexts collaborate through explicit domain events wherever appropriate.

This minimizes coupling while supporting future scalability.

---

## Principle 10 — Evolutionary Architecture

The application architecture shall support continuous evolution through modularity, versioning, and explicit architectural boundaries.

Future neuro-symbolic components shall integrate through existing ports rather than modifying the domain model.

---

# 19.6 Chapter Transition

Having established the motivation, objectives, architectural context, Domain-Driven Design philosophy, and guiding principles for the application architecture, the next section introduces the **Strategic Domain Model**. This model identifies the core business domains of NutriLogic, defines their relationships, and establishes the bounded contexts that form the foundation of the application's software structure.

# 19.7 Strategic Domain Model

## 19.7.1 Introduction

The Strategic Domain Model provides the highest-level representation of NutriLogic's business capabilities. Rather than focusing on software modules or technical infrastructure, it identifies the major problem domains that collectively implement the Nutrition Care Process (NCP), symbolic reasoning, knowledge management, and longitudinal patient care.

Within Domain-Driven Design (DDD), strategic modelling establishes the boundaries within which specific terminology, rules, and responsibilities remain internally consistent. These boundaries, known as **Bounded Contexts**, enable each domain to evolve independently while collaborating through explicit interfaces.

For NutriLogic, the Strategic Domain Model is informed by four complementary perspectives:

* The Nutrition Care Process (NCP)
* Knowledge Engineering principles
* Clinical Decision Support System (CDSS) architecture
* Enterprise software engineering best practices

Consequently, the application architecture reflects the structure of clinical nutrition practice rather than the implementation details of Django, PostgreSQL, or SWI-Prolog.

---

## 19.7.2 Domain Classification

Not every domain contributes equally to the business objectives of the platform. Following Domain-Driven Design, the domains are classified into **Core Domains**, **Supporting Domains**, and **Generic Domains**.

### Core Domains

Core Domains embody the unique intellectual property and research contribution of NutriLogic. They contain the clinical reasoning, semantic knowledge representation, and explainability mechanisms that differentiate the platform from conventional nutrition applications.

The Core Domains are:

* Assessment
* Knowledge
* Inference
* Intervention
* Explainability

These domains collectively realize the Nutrition Care Process and the symbolic reasoning workflow.

---

### Supporting Domains

Supporting Domains facilitate the operation of the Core Domains but do not directly perform clinical reasoning.

These include:

* Monitoring
* Analytics

Monitoring enables longitudinal patient follow-up, while Analytics provides insights into system usage, recommendation effectiveness, and population-level nutritional trends.

---

### Generic Domains

Generic Domains implement common enterprise capabilities that are not unique to clinical nutrition but are essential for a secure and functional platform.

These include:

* Users and Identity Management
* Authentication and Authorization
* Notifications
* Audit Logging
* File Management
* Configuration

Where possible, these domains should leverage mature frameworks and libraries rather than bespoke implementations.

---

## 19.7.3 Strategic Domain Hierarchy

The hierarchical relationship among the domains is illustrated below.

```text
                    NutriLogic Platform
                            │
      ┌─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┐
      │                                           │
Core Clinical Domains                   Enterprise Support Domains
      │                                           │
      │                                   Users & Identity
      │                                   Notifications
      │                                   Configuration
      │                                   Audit Logging
      │
      ├───────────────┬───────────────────────┐
      │               │                       │
 Assessment      Knowledge             Explainability
      │               │                       │
      └──────┬────────┴───────────────┐
             │                        │
         Inference              Intervention
             │
             ▼
        Monitoring
             │
             ▼
         Analytics
```

The hierarchy illustrates that the Knowledge Domain forms the semantic foundation of the platform, while the Assessment and Inference domains orchestrate the clinical reasoning workflow. Explainability operates as an independent domain, consuming outputs from both the Knowledge and Inference domains to generate transparent, evidence-based explanations.

---

# 19.8 Bounded Contexts

## 19.8.1 Overview

Each Bounded Context encapsulates a cohesive business capability with clearly defined responsibilities, terminology, and interfaces. No bounded context directly manipulates the internal implementation of another; collaboration occurs through explicit contracts, domain events, or application services.

This approach minimizes coupling, improves maintainability, and enables future architectural evolution.

---

## 19.8.2 Users and Identity Context

### Purpose

Manage user identities, authentication, authorization, and longitudinal health profiles.

### Responsibilities

* User registration
* Authentication (JWT)
* Role management
* Patient demographics
* Anthropometric baseline
* Clinical history
* Consent management
* Privacy preferences

### Principal Entities

* User
* HealthProfile
* ConsentRecord
* Role

---

## 19.8.3 Assessment Context

### Purpose

Capture and validate all information required during the Assessment phase of the Nutrition Care Process.

### Responsibilities

* Anthropometric assessment
* Clinical observations
* Dietary assessment
* Lifestyle assessment
* Symptom capture
* Laboratory observations
* Nutritional screening
* Assessment validation

### Aggregate Root

NutritionAssessment

### Primary Value Objects

* Age
* Height
* Weight
* BMI
* BloodPressure
* WaistCircumference
* PhysicalActivityLevel
* DietaryDiversityScore
* FoodFrequencyProfile

---

## 19.8.4 Knowledge Context

### Purpose

Serve as the semantic foundation of NutriLogic by managing the ontology, knowledge graph, symbolic knowledge base, and clinical guideline mappings.

### Responsibilities

* Nutrition ontology management
* Knowledge graph maintenance
* Rule versioning
* Knowledge provenance
* Guideline synchronization
* Knowledge validation
* Rule lifecycle management
* Ontology evolution

### Aggregate Root

KnowledgeModule

### Principal Entities

* NutritionConcept
* FoodConcept
* NutrientConcept
* ClinicalGuideline
* RuleSet
* OntologyVersion

Unlike conventional applications, knowledge itself is treated as a first-class business asset rather than a static infrastructure resource.

---

## 19.8.5 Inference Context

### Purpose

Coordinate symbolic reasoning by translating clinical observations into symbolic facts and orchestrating communication with the SWI-Prolog reasoning engine.

### Responsibilities

* Fact generation
* Predicate mapping
* Query execution
* Working memory management
* Rule invocation
* Reasoning trace generation
* Conflict resolution
* Inference orchestration

### Aggregate Root

InferenceSession

### Principal Entities

* InferenceRequest
* InferenceTrace
* WorkingMemory
* RuleActivation

The Inference Context functions as an Anti-Corruption Layer between the object-oriented application and the symbolic reasoning subsystem.

---

## 19.8.6 Intervention Context

### Purpose

Manage nutrition interventions and personalized recommendations generated through symbolic reasoning.

### Responsibilities

* Recommendation generation
* Meal plan construction
* Dietary advice
* Goal formulation
* Therapeutic interventions
* Behavioural guidance

### Aggregate Root

RecommendationPlan

### Principal Entities

* Recommendation
* MealPlan
* NutritionGoal
* DietaryIntervention

---

## 19.8.7 Explainability Context

### Purpose

Generate transparent, traceable, and clinically meaningful explanations that justify every recommendation produced by the reasoning engine.

### Responsibilities

* Rule trace visualization
* Evidence aggregation
* Provenance tracking
* Confidence estimation
* Natural language explanation generation
* Recommendation justification
* Clinical evidence linking

### Aggregate Root

ExplanationSession

### Principal Entities

* Explanation
* EvidenceChain
* RuleTrace
* ConfidenceProfile

Treating explainability as a dedicated domain reflects its central importance in trustworthy clinical decision support.

---

## 19.8.8 Monitoring Context

### Purpose

Support longitudinal monitoring of patient outcomes and adherence following intervention.

### Responsibilities

* Follow-up assessments
* Behaviour tracking
* Goal adherence
* Progress evaluation
* Outcome recording
* Clinical alerts

### Aggregate Root

MonitoringRecord

---

## 19.8.9 Analytics Context

### Purpose

Provide aggregated insights for clinicians, researchers, and administrators without influencing individual clinical decisions.

### Responsibilities

* Population nutrition trends
* Recommendation effectiveness
* Clinical dashboards
* County-level statistics
* Research reporting
* Performance analytics
* Quality improvement metrics

Analytics is intentionally separated from the reasoning process to preserve deterministic clinical decision-making while supporting evidence generation and service improvement.

---

# 19.9 Context Map

While Bounded Contexts define architectural boundaries, the Context Map specifies how these domains collaborate to realize the end-to-end clinical workflow.

```text
                          Users & Identity
                                 │
                                 ▼
                          Assessment Context
                                 │
                  AssessmentCompleted Event
                                 │
                                 ▼
                        Inference Context
                        ▲               │
                        │               ▼
                Knowledge Context   Explainability Context
                        │               │
                        └───────┬───────┘
                                ▼
                     Intervention Context
                                │
                                ▼
                      Monitoring Context
                                │
                                ▼
                       Analytics Context
```

The Context Map illustrates an event-driven collaboration model in which:

* **Assessment** initiates the clinical workflow.
* **Inference** consults the **Knowledge Context** while generating recommendations.
* **Explainability** consumes inference traces and knowledge provenance to construct transparent explanations.
* **Intervention** delivers clinically actionable recommendations.
* **Monitoring** evaluates longitudinal patient outcomes.
* **Analytics** derives aggregate insights without participating in patient-specific clinical reasoning.

---

# 19.10 Strategic Design Decisions

The Strategic Domain Model embodies several architectural decisions that guide future implementation.

**Knowledge as a Core Domain.** The ontology, knowledge graph, and symbolic rule base are treated as business assets rather than infrastructure artefacts, emphasizing that knowledge management is central to NutriLogic's purpose.

**Explainability as an Independent Capability.** Recommendation explanations are modelled as a dedicated domain, enabling transparent clinical reasoning, regulatory compliance, and user trust.

**Event-Driven Collaboration.** Bounded Contexts communicate primarily through domain events, reducing coupling and facilitating future asynchronous processing.

**Infrastructure Independence.** Domain models remain isolated from Django, PostgreSQL, and SWI-Prolog, ensuring that technological changes do not alter the conceptual model.

**Evolutionary Modularity.** Each bounded context may evolve independently and, if necessary, be extracted into a separate deployable service without fundamentally changing the domain model.

---

# 19.11 Chapter Transition

Having established the Strategic Domain Model, identified the Bounded Contexts, and defined their interactions through the Context Map, the next part of this chapter focuses on the tactical design elements within each context. Specifically, it introduces Aggregate Roots, Entities, Value Objects, and Domain Services that collectively implement the behaviour of each bounded context while preserving consistency, encapsulation, and domain integrity.

# 19.12 Tactical Domain Model

## 19.12.1 Introduction

While the Strategic Domain Model identifies the major business capabilities of NutriLogic, Tactical Domain Design defines the internal composition of those capabilities. It specifies how clinical concepts are represented in software while preserving consistency, encapsulation, and domain integrity.

The tactical model follows the principles of Domain-Driven Design (DDD), employing:

* Aggregate Roots
* Entities
* Value Objects
* Domain Services
* Application Services
* Repositories
* Domain Events

Together, these constructs ensure that the software mirrors the clinical nutrition domain rather than the underlying implementation technologies.

---

# 19.13 Aggregate Architecture

## 19.13.1 Aggregate Design

An **Aggregate** is a cluster of related domain objects treated as a single unit for consistency and transactional integrity.

Each aggregate has exactly one **Aggregate Root**, which serves as the only external entry point into the aggregate.

This architectural constraint prevents inconsistent modifications and protects domain invariants.

The aggregate architecture adopted for NutriLogic is illustrated below.

```text
                   Users
                     │
          Aggregate Root
                   User
                     │
     ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐
     │                               │
HealthProfile                      Role
     │
     ├────────────────┐
     │                │
Demographics    MedicalHistory
```

---

## 19.13.2 Assessment Aggregate

The Assessment Aggregate encapsulates all information collected during the Assessment phase of the Nutrition Care Process.

### Aggregate Root

```text
NutritionAssessment
```

### Child Entities

* AnthropometricObservation
* ClinicalObservation
* DietaryObservation
* LifestyleObservation
* LaboratoryObservation
* AssessmentFact

### Value Objects

* Height
* Weight
* BMI
* WaistCircumference
* BloodPressure
* Age
* Sex
* PhysicalActivityLevel
* DietaryDiversityScore
* FoodFrequencyProfile

Only the `NutritionAssessment` aggregate root may modify the aggregate, ensuring that every assessment remains clinically consistent.

---

## 19.13.3 Knowledge Aggregate

The Knowledge Aggregate represents the semantic assets underpinning NutriLogic.

### Aggregate Root

```text
KnowledgeModule
```

### Child Entities

* OntologyVersion
* RuleSet
* GuidelineMapping
* KnowledgeSource
* KnowledgeGraphFragment

### Value Objects

* ConceptIdentifier
* Predicate
* RuleIdentifier
* OntologyIRI
* GuidelineReference
* ProvenanceRecord

The Knowledge Aggregate guarantees that modifications to ontology versions, symbolic rules, and guideline mappings remain synchronized.

---

## 19.13.4 Inference Aggregate

The Inference Aggregate manages a complete reasoning session.

### Aggregate Root

```text
InferenceSession
```

### Child Entities

* WorkingMemory
* RuleActivation
* ConflictSet
* InferenceTrace
* ExplanationRequest

### Value Objects

* QueryIdentifier
* Predicate
* Fact
* ConfidenceScore
* ExecutionTime

Each inference session represents an immutable reasoning episode whose execution can be audited and reproduced.

---

## 19.13.5 Recommendation Aggregate

### Aggregate Root

```text
RecommendationPlan
```

### Child Entities

* Recommendation
* MealPlan
* NutritionGoal
* BehaviouralAdvice
* ReferralRecommendation

### Value Objects

* PortionSize
* ServingFrequency
* NutrientTarget
* EnergyRequirement
* ProteinRequirement

---

## 19.13.6 Monitoring Aggregate

### Aggregate Root

```text
MonitoringRecord
```

### Child Entities

* FollowUpAssessment
* BehaviourTracking
* ProgressEvaluation
* OutcomeMeasure

### Value Objects

* ProgressScore
* AdherenceLevel
* MonitoringInterval

---

# 19.14 Domain Entities

Entities represent domain concepts possessing a stable identity throughout their lifecycle.

Unlike Value Objects, two entities remain distinct even when their attributes are identical.

Examples include:

| Domain         | Entity              |
| -------------- | ------------------- |
| Users          | User                |
| Assessment     | NutritionAssessment |
| Knowledge      | RuleSet             |
| Knowledge      | NutritionConcept    |
| Inference      | InferenceSession    |
| Intervention   | Recommendation      |
| Monitoring     | MonitoringRecord    |
| Explainability | Explanation         |

Each entity encapsulates its own business behaviour and is responsible for preserving its internal consistency.

Entities should not merely serve as data containers; instead, they model the behaviour and lifecycle of clinical concepts.

---

# 19.15 Value Objects

## 19.15.1 Overview

Value Objects represent immutable domain concepts defined entirely by their attributes rather than identity.

They simplify modelling by eliminating unnecessary identifiers and preventing accidental mutation.

NutriLogic employs Value Objects extensively because clinical measurements are naturally immutable observations.

Examples include:

### Anthropometric Measurements

* Height
* Weight
* BMI
* MidUpperArmCircumference
* WaistCircumference

---

### Physiological Measurements

* BloodPressure
* HeartRate
* BloodGlucose
* BodyTemperature

---

### Nutrition Measurements

* DailyEnergyRequirement
* MacronutrientProfile
* MicronutrientRequirement
* DietaryDiversityScore
* PortionSize
* ServingFrequency

---

### Knowledge Objects

* Predicate
* RuleIdentifier
* ConceptIdentifier
* OntologyIRI
* GuidelineReference

---

### Explainability Objects

* ConfidenceScore
* EvidenceStrength
* ExplanationLevel
* ProvenanceRecord

Value Objects simplify equality comparisons, improve domain expressiveness, and reinforce immutability across the application.

---

# 19.16 Domain Services

## 19.16.1 Motivation

Certain business operations do not naturally belong to a single entity.

Placing such behaviour inside entities would violate cohesion and obscure business responsibilities.

These operations are modelled as **Domain Services**.

---

## 19.16.2 Assessment Domain Services

Examples include:

* NutritionRiskAssessmentService
* NutritionalStatusClassifier
* AnthropometricInterpreter
* DietaryPatternAnalyzer

These services coordinate multiple entities while preserving domain integrity.

---

## 19.16.3 Knowledge Domain Services

Examples include:

* OntologyValidationService
* RuleConsistencyChecker
* GuidelineSynchronizer
* KnowledgeVersionManager

These services ensure that the knowledge base remains logically coherent and clinically aligned.

---

## 19.16.4 Inference Domain Services

Examples include:

* FactGenerator
* PredicateMapper
* ConflictResolutionService
* WorkingMemoryManager
* ExplanationCollector

These services prepare symbolic facts, coordinate reasoning, and collect inference traces without embedding Prolog-specific concerns into the domain model.

---

## 19.16.5 Intervention Domain Services

Examples include:

* MealPlanOptimizer
* NutrientRequirementCalculator
* RecommendationPrioritizer
* ContraindicationChecker

These services encapsulate complex clinical computations and recommendation logic.

---

## 19.16.6 Explainability Domain Services

Examples include:

* EvidenceAggregator
* RuleTraceBuilder
* NaturalLanguageExplanationGenerator
* ConfidenceEstimator
* ProvenanceResolver

By isolating explanation logic within dedicated services, the platform supports transparent and reusable reasoning narratives.

---

# 19.17 Application Services

Whereas Domain Services encapsulate domain-specific behaviour, **Application Services** coordinate complete use cases that span multiple bounded contexts.

Application Services:

* orchestrate workflows,
* manage transactions,
* invoke repositories,
* publish domain events,
* coordinate communication with infrastructure.

They do **not** implement business rules themselves.

Representative Application Services include:

| Application Service              | Primary Responsibility                 |
| -------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| AssessmentApplicationService     | Coordinate nutrition assessments       |
| InferenceApplicationService      | Execute symbolic reasoning workflow    |
| RecommendationApplicationService | Generate personalized interventions    |
| ExplanationApplicationService    | Produce explainable recommendations    |
| MonitoringApplicationService     | Manage longitudinal follow-up          |
| KnowledgeApplicationService      | Coordinate ontology and rule lifecycle |

This separation between Domain Services and Application Services maintains a clear distinction between **business logic** and **workflow orchestration**, improving maintainability and testability.

---

# 19.18 Tactical Design Decisions

The tactical domain model reflects several important architectural decisions:

* **Aggregate Roots** enforce consistency boundaries and protect domain invariants.
* **Entities** model long-lived clinical concepts whose identity persists over time.
* **Value Objects** represent immutable clinical observations and semantic concepts.
* **Domain Services** encapsulate complex business behaviour spanning multiple entities.
* **Application Services** coordinate workflows without embedding business rules.
* **Immutable clinical observations** improve auditability and reduce unintended side effects.
* **Semantic concepts** (e.g., predicates, ontology identifiers, guideline references) are modelled as first-class Value Objects to strengthen integration with the symbolic reasoning subsystem.

---

# 19.19 Chapter Transition

The tactical domain model establishes the internal structure of each bounded context, defining how clinical concepts, semantic knowledge, and reasoning artefacts are represented and protected within the application core. Building upon this foundation, the next part of the chapter introduces the architectural mechanisms that connect the domain to external technologies, including Repository Architecture, Ports and Adapters, and the Anti-Corruption Layer that mediates communication between the object-oriented application and the SWI-Prolog symbolic reasoning engine.

# 19.20 Repository Architecture

## 19.20.1 Introduction

Repositories provide the boundary between the domain model and persistence infrastructure. Rather than allowing domain objects to directly manipulate database technologies such as the Django ORM, repositories abstract data access behind well-defined interfaces.

Within NutriLogic, repositories serve two complementary purposes.

First, they isolate the application core from persistence mechanisms, ensuring that domain entities remain persistence ignorant.

Second, they translate between rich domain models and database representations, allowing each layer to evolve independently.

This architectural approach conforms to both the Repository Pattern and the Dependency Inversion Principle.

---

## 19.20.2 Repository Responsibilities

Repositories are responsible for:

* Retrieving Aggregate Roots from persistent storage.
* Persisting Aggregate Roots after successful validation.
* Mapping between Domain Entities and Django ORM models.
* Enforcing transactional consistency.
* Supporting optimistic concurrency where appropriate.
* Hiding persistence technology from the domain.

Repositories **shall not** contain business rules.

Clinical validation belongs within the Domain Layer.

Workflow orchestration belongs within Application Services.

---

## 19.20.3 Repository Hierarchy

```text
                           Repository Interfaces
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

IAssessmentRepository

IKnowledgeRepository

IInferenceRepository

IRecommendationRepository

IMonitoringRepository

IUserRepository

IExplanationRepository

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
                     ▲
                     │
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

DjangoAssessmentRepository

DjangoKnowledgeRepository

DjangoInferenceRepository

DjangoRecommendationRepository

DjangoMonitoringRepository

DjangoUserRepository

DjangoExplanationRepository

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
                     │
                     ▼
                 Django ORM
                     │
               PostgreSQL Database
```

The application layer depends exclusively upon repository interfaces, while infrastructure provides the concrete implementations.

---

## 19.20.4 Repository Example

```python
class IAssessmentRepository(ABC):

    @abstractmethod
    def save(self, assessment: NutritionAssessment) -> None:
        pass

    @abstractmethod
    def find_by_id(self, assessment_id: UUID) -> NutritionAssessment:
        pass

    @abstractmethod
    def find_by_patient(self, patient_id: UUID):
        pass
```

The domain is therefore unaware of Django ORM queries, SQL statements, or database transactions.

---

# 19.21 Ports and Adapters Architecture

## 19.21.1 Motivation

NutriLogic integrates several heterogeneous technologies, including:

* SWI-Prolog
* PostgreSQL
* Redis
* JWT Authentication
* React
* External guideline repositories
* Future machine learning services

Allowing these technologies to directly interact with domain models would tightly couple business logic to infrastructure.

Instead, the architecture adopts **Hexagonal Architecture (Ports and Adapters)**.

---

## 19.21.2 Hexagonal Architecture

```text
                    External World

        React       PostgreSQL      SWI-Prolog

             \          |           /

              \         |          /

────────────────────────────────────────────────────
               Infrastructure Adapters
────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 REST Adapter

 Repository Adapter

 Prolog Adapter

 Authentication Adapter

 Notification Adapter

────────────────────────────────────────────────────
                  Application Ports
────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Assessment Port

 Inference Port

 Knowledge Port

 Monitoring Port

 Recommendation Port

────────────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Domain Model
────────────────────────────────────────────────────
```

The Domain Model communicates only through ports.

Infrastructure technologies implement those ports via adapters.

---

## 19.21.3 Primary Ports

Primary (driving) ports represent operations initiated by external actors.

Examples include:

* SubmitAssessment
* GenerateRecommendation
* UpdateMonitoringRecord
* RetrieveExplanation
* UpdateKnowledgeModule

These ports are invoked by REST controllers, scheduled tasks, or future GraphQL interfaces.

---

## 19.21.4 Secondary Ports

Secondary (driven) ports represent services required by the domain.

Examples include:

* IAssessmentRepository
* IInferenceEngine
* IKnowledgeRepository
* INotificationService
* IAuditService

Infrastructure supplies concrete implementations for these abstractions.

---

# 19.22 Anti-Corruption Layer (ACL)

## 19.22.1 Motivation

One of the defining characteristics of NutriLogic is its integration of object-oriented software with symbolic reasoning implemented in SWI-Prolog.

These technologies employ fundamentally different computational paradigms.

Python models information as objects.

Prolog models knowledge as logical predicates.

Allowing these paradigms to directly depend upon one another would create tight coupling and significantly reduce maintainability.

Consequently, the architecture introduces an **Anti-Corruption Layer (ACL)** between the application domain and the symbolic reasoning engine.

---

## 19.22.2 Architectural Position

```text
                    Domain Model
                          │
                  Domain Entities
                          │
────────────────────────────────────────────
            Anti-Corruption Layer
────────────────────────────────────────────

 Fact Translator

 Predicate Mapper

 Rule Formatter

 Result Translator

 Explanation Mapper

────────────────────────────────────────────
                          │
                   SWI-Prolog Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────
```

The ACL protects both the object-oriented application and the symbolic reasoning engine from leaking implementation details into one another.

---

## 19.22.3 Responsibilities

The ACL is responsible for:

* Translating domain entities into symbolic facts.
* Constructing Prolog predicates.
* Executing reasoning queries.
* Translating Prolog solutions into domain entities.
* Capturing reasoning traces.
* Mapping explanations.
* Validating predicate syntax.
* Isolating PySWIP-specific implementation details.

This design ensures that no application service directly imports or invokes PySWIP.

---

## 19.22.4 Translation Example

Domain Entity

```text
NutritionAssessment
```

↓

Fact Translation

```prolog
age(patient_001,45).
bmi(patient_001,31.4).
condition(patient_001,hypertension).
```

↓

Reasoning

```prolog
recommend(patient_001,R).
```

↓

Translated Result

```text
RecommendationPlan
```

The translation process is entirely encapsulated within the ACL.

---

# 19.23 Dependency Rules

Maintaining architectural integrity requires explicit dependency constraints.

The following rules govern all software development within NutriLogic.

---

## Rule 1

The Domain Layer shall never import infrastructure packages.

---

## Rule 2

Repositories shall implement domain interfaces.

The reverse dependency is prohibited.

---

## Rule 3

REST Controllers shall invoke Application Services only.

Controllers shall never manipulate repositories directly.

---

## Rule 4

Application Services shall coordinate workflows but shall not implement clinical business rules.

---

## Rule 5

Only the Anti-Corruption Layer may communicate with PySWIP.

---

## Rule 6

Only Aggregate Roots may modify aggregate state.

---

## Rule 7

Domain Entities shall not contain SQL, ORM queries, HTTP requests, or framework annotations.

---

## Rule 8

Knowledge Modules shall never depend upon presentation technologies.

---

## Rule 9

Machine Learning components introduced in future neuro-symbolic extensions shall communicate through existing inference ports rather than bypassing the symbolic reasoning architecture.

---

# 19.24 Dependency Diagram

```text
Presentation Layer
        │
        ▼
Application Layer
        │
        ▼
Domain Layer
        │
        ▼
Application Ports
        │
        ▼
Infrastructure Adapters
        │
        ▼
External Technologies
```

Notice the dependency direction always points **toward the Domain Layer**, ensuring that business knowledge remains insulated from implementation technologies.

---

# 19.25 Architecture Fitness Rules

To preserve architectural integrity over time, the following fitness rules shall be continuously verified during development and code review.

| Rule                 | Expected Constraint                                                   |
| -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Domain imports       | No imports from `django.*`, `pyswip`, or infrastructure packages      |
| Repository usage     | Only Application Services invoke repositories                         |
| Controller behaviour | Controllers contain no business logic                                 |
| Aggregate integrity  | Child entities are modified only through Aggregate Roots              |
| Prolog interaction   | All reasoning passes through the Anti-Corruption Layer                |
| Knowledge isolation  | Ontology and rule modules remain independent of presentation concerns |
| Testability          | Domain services can execute without Django or PostgreSQL              |
| Event publication    | Cross-context communication uses Domain Events where appropriate      |

These fitness rules can later be enforced through architectural tests and continuous integration pipelines, helping to prevent architectural erosion as the platform evolves.

---

# 19.26 Chapter Transition

The repository architecture, ports and adapters, dependency rules, and Anti-Corruption Layer collectively establish the mechanisms through which the domain model interacts with external technologies while preserving architectural integrity. The next section builds upon these foundations by introducing the **Domain Event Architecture**, workflow orchestration, interface contracts, and quality attribute scenarios that govern runtime behaviour across the NutriLogic platform.

---

# 19.27 Domain Event Architecture

## 19.27.1 Introduction

A modern Clinical Nutrition Decision Support System consists of multiple collaborating domains whose activities must remain coordinated without introducing unnecessary coupling. Direct dependencies between bounded contexts increase architectural complexity, reduce maintainability, and make future evolution difficult.

To address this challenge, NutriLogic adopts an **Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)** within the application layer. Instead of invoking one another directly, bounded contexts communicate through **Domain Events**, which represent significant occurrences within the business domain.

A Domain Event records **something that has already happened** and may trigger reactions in one or more collaborating contexts. Events are immutable, timestamped, and traceable, supporting both operational workflows and clinical audit requirements.

---

## 19.27.2 Event Design Principles

The Domain Event Architecture is governed by the following principles:

* Events describe completed business facts rather than commands.
* Events are immutable once published.
* Publishing an event does not assume how many subscribers exist.
* Bounded contexts communicate through events rather than direct dependencies wherever appropriate.
* Events contribute to auditability and traceability by recording significant domain transitions.
* Event publication shall not expose infrastructure-specific implementation details.

These principles promote loose coupling while preserving consistency across the application.

---

## 19.27.3 Event Lifecycle

The lifecycle of a Domain Event is illustrated below.

```text
Business Operation
        │
        ▼
Business Rule Validated
        │
        ▼
Domain Event Created
        │
        ▼
Event Published
        │
        ▼
Interested Contexts Subscribe
        │
        ▼
Application Services Execute
        │
        ▼
New Domain Events (Optional)
```

The event lifecycle enables multiple domains to react independently to a single business occurrence without requiring direct knowledge of one another.

---

## 19.27.4 Core Domain Events

The following Domain Events form the backbone of the NutriLogic application workflow.

| Event                   | Publisher      | Typical Subscribers          |
| ----------------------- | -------------- | ---------------------------- |
| UserRegistered          | Users Context  | Monitoring, Analytics        |
| ProfileUpdated          | Users Context  | Assessment                   |
| AssessmentSubmitted     | Assessment     | Inference                    |
| AssessmentValidated     | Assessment     | Inference                    |
| InferenceStarted        | Inference      | Audit                        |
| InferenceCompleted      | Inference      | Explainability, Intervention |
| RecommendationGenerated | Intervention   | Monitoring, Analytics        |
| ExplanationGenerated    | Explainability | API Layer                    |
| FollowUpScheduled       | Monitoring     | Notifications                |
| MonitoringUpdated       | Monitoring     | Analytics                    |
| KnowledgeModuleUpdated  | Knowledge      | Inference                    |
| OntologyVersionReleased | Knowledge      | Knowledge Cache              |
| RuleApproved            | Knowledge      | Inference                    |
| RuleDeprecated          | Knowledge      | Audit                        |

This event catalogue provides a standardized vocabulary for runtime communication.

---

# 19.28 Application Workflow Orchestration

## 19.28.1 Overview

Application Services orchestrate complete business workflows by coordinating multiple bounded contexts. They invoke repositories, publish domain events, and interact with infrastructure through ports and adapters.

Importantly, they **do not** implement clinical business rules. Those remain within the Domain Layer.

---

## 19.28.2 Clinical Recommendation Workflow

The principal application workflow is illustrated below.

```text
Patient Assessment
        │
        ▼
Assessment Application Service
        │
        ▼
Assessment Repository
        │
        ▼
AssessmentSubmitted Event
        │
        ▼
Inference Application Service
        │
        ▼
Inference Port
        │
        ▼
Anti-Corruption Layer
        │
        ▼
SWI-Prolog Reasoning Engine
        │
        ▼
Inference Results
        │
        ▼
Explanation Application Service
        │
        ▼
Recommendation Application Service
        │
        ▼
Recommendation Repository
        │
        ▼
Monitoring Context
        │
        ▼
Analytics Context
```

This orchestration demonstrates how application services coordinate the end-to-end workflow while preserving the independence of each bounded context.

---

## 19.28.3 Knowledge Evolution Workflow

Clinical knowledge evolves over time as new guidelines, nutrient profile models, or expert consensus become available.

The corresponding workflow is shown below.

```text
Updated Clinical Guideline
          │
          ▼
Knowledge Application Service
          │
          ▼
Ontology Update
          │
          ▼
Knowledge Graph Update
          │
          ▼
Rule Validation
          │
          ▼
Rule Approval
          │
          ▼
KnowledgeModuleUpdated Event
          │
          ▼
Inference Context Refresh
```

This workflow supports controlled evolution of the knowledge base while maintaining clinical governance.

---

# 19.29 Interface Contracts

## 19.29.1 Purpose

Application components communicate through explicit contracts rather than concrete implementations.

These contracts define:

* accepted inputs,
* expected outputs,
* operational behaviour,
* exceptional conditions,
* invariants.

This approach promotes loose coupling and enables infrastructure technologies to be replaced without affecting the application core.

---

## 19.29.2 Inference Interface

```python
class IInferenceEngine(ABC):

    @abstractmethod
    def execute(
        self,
        assessment: NutritionAssessment
    ) -> RecommendationPlan:
        """Execute symbolic reasoning."""
```

Implementations may include:

* PrologInferenceAdapter
* MockInferenceAdapter (testing)
* FutureNeuroSymbolicAdapter

---

## 19.29.3 Knowledge Repository Interface

```python
class IKnowledgeRepository(ABC):

    @abstractmethod
    def get_active_rules(self):
        ...

    @abstractmethod
    def get_current_ontology(self):
        ...

    @abstractmethod
    def save_rules(self):
        ...
```

The interface defines the behaviour expected by the application while concealing implementation details such as databases or external knowledge sources.

---

# 19.30 Dependency Injection Strategy

To preserve inversion of control, dependencies are supplied to application services through constructor injection.

Illustrative example:

```text
RecommendationApplicationService
        │
        ├── IAssessmentRepository
        ├── IInferenceEngine
        ├── IRecommendationRepository
        ├── IExplanationService
        └── IEventPublisher
```

This strategy improves modularity, simplifies testing, and enables infrastructure components to be substituted without modifying application logic.

---

# 19.31 Quality Attribute Scenarios

## 19.31.1 Introduction

While Chapter 18 identified the quality attributes guiding the architecture, this section expresses them as **Quality Attribute Scenarios**, a common architecture evaluation technique. Each scenario describes a measurable expectation for how the application should respond under specific conditions.

---

### Maintainability

| Attribute   | Scenario                                                                                                             |
| ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Source      | Knowledge Engineer                                                                                                   |
| Stimulus    | New Ministry of Health dietary guideline                                                                             |
| Environment | Development                                                                                                          |
| Response    | Add or revise ontology concepts and Prolog rules without modifying application services or user interface components |
| Measure     | Existing functionality continues to pass regression tests                                                            |

---

### Performance

| Attribute   | Scenario                                                                                        |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Source      | Patient                                                                                         |
| Stimulus    | Submit a nutrition assessment                                                                   |
| Environment | Normal operation                                                                                |
| Response    | Assessment is validated, reasoning executed, explanation generated, and recommendation returned |
| Measure     | End-to-end response time remains within project performance targets                             |

---

### Modifiability

| Attribute   | Scenario                                                     |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Source      | Software Engineer                                            |
| Stimulus    | Introduce a wearable-device integration                      |
| Environment | System evolution                                             |
| Response    | Implement a new adapter without changing the domain model    |
| Measure     | Existing bounded contexts require minimal or no modification |

---

### Reliability

| Attribute   | Scenario                                                                                      |
| ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Source      | Infrastructure                                                                                |
| Stimulus    | Temporary failure of the symbolic reasoning engine                                            |
| Environment | Runtime                                                                                       |
| Response    | Error is captured, logged, surfaced appropriately, and no partial clinical state is persisted |
| Measure     | Data integrity and auditability are preserved                                                 |

---

### Explainability

| Attribute   | Scenario                                                                                              |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Source      | Clinician                                                                                             |
| Stimulus    | Review a recommendation                                                                               |
| Environment | Clinical consultation                                                                                 |
| Response    | System displays triggered rules, supporting evidence, guideline references, and explanation narrative |
| Measure     | Every recommendation is traceable to the knowledge and reasoning that produced it                     |

---

# 19.32 Architecture Decision Record

## ADR-018 — Event-Driven Application Collaboration

### Status

Accepted

### Context

Direct invocation between bounded contexts creates unnecessary coupling and complicates future evolution of the application.

### Decision

Adopt an Event-Driven Architecture for collaboration among bounded contexts, using immutable Domain Events published by Application Services.

### Rationale

* Supports loose coupling.
* Improves scalability.
* Enhances auditability.
* Aligns with Domain-Driven Design.
* Facilitates asynchronous processing where appropriate.

### Consequences

The application introduces event publication and subscription mechanisms, increasing implementation complexity but providing greater architectural flexibility and long-term maintainability.

# Chapter 19: Domain-Driven Application Architecture

# Part VI — Implementation Strategy, CQRS, Technology Mapping, and Architectural Governance

---

# 19.34 Implementation Strategy

## 19.34.1 Introduction

The Domain-Driven Application Architecture presented in this chapter defines the conceptual structure of NutriLogic. Its implementation, however, requires translating these concepts into a maintainable software organization while preserving architectural integrity.

Rather than implementing the system as a conventional Django project with tightly coupled models, views, and serializers, NutriLogic adopts a layered implementation strategy in which the domain model remains independent of framework concerns. Django provides infrastructure services—HTTP handling, authentication, ORM, and administrative utilities—while the application core encapsulates the clinical workflows and neuro-symbolic orchestration.

This implementation strategy aligns with the principles of Clean Architecture, Domain-Driven Design (DDD), and Hexagonal Architecture introduced earlier in the chapter.

---

## 19.34.2 Implementation Layers

The implementation is organized into five principal layers.

```text
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   Presentation Layer                          │
│ React • REST Controllers • DRF Serializers • Authentication   │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           │
                           ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  Application Layer                            │
│ Commands • Queries • Application Services • Event Handlers    │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           │
                           ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     Domain Layer                              │
│ Aggregates • Entities • Value Objects • Domain Services       │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           │
                           ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 Infrastructure Layer                          │
│ Django ORM • PostgreSQL • Redis • PySWIP • External APIs      │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           │
                           ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Knowledge Infrastructure Layer                   │
│ Ontology • Knowledge Graph • SWI-Prolog • Rule Base           │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Each layer depends only on abstractions exposed by the layer immediately below it, ensuring that the domain model remains insulated from implementation technologies.

---

# 19.35 Lightweight Command–Query Responsibility Segregation (CQRS)

## 19.35.1 Motivation

Clinical decision support systems perform two fundamentally different categories of operations:

1. **State-changing operations**, such as submitting an assessment or approving a new rule.
2. **Information retrieval operations**, such as viewing recommendation history or retrieving patient dashboards.

Treating both categories identically often results in bloated service classes and blurred responsibilities. To improve clarity without introducing unnecessary architectural complexity, NutriLogic adopts **lightweight Command–Query Responsibility Segregation (CQRS)**.

Unlike full CQRS implementations, this approach:

* uses a single PostgreSQL database,
* avoids event sourcing,
* shares the same domain model,
* separates application logic rather than persistence.

This provides many of the organizational benefits of CQRS while remaining appropriate for the project's scope.

---

## 19.35.2 Commands

Commands represent requests that modify application state.

Typical commands include:

| Command                | Responsibility                         |
| ---------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| RegisterUser           | Create a new user account              |
| UpdateHealthProfile    | Modify demographic or clinical profile |
| SubmitAssessment       | Persist a nutrition assessment         |
| ExecuteInference       | Initiate symbolic reasoning            |
| GenerateRecommendation | Produce personalized interventions     |
| ApproveKnowledgeModule | Approve updated clinical knowledge     |
| ScheduleFollowUp       | Create monitoring activities           |
| RecordOutcome          | Persist monitoring results             |

Each command is processed by a dedicated Command Handler responsible for coordinating validation, domain services, repositories, and event publication.

---

## 19.35.3 Queries

Queries retrieve information without modifying application state.

Representative queries include:

| Query                    | Returned Information           |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------ |
| GetPatientProfile        | Longitudinal patient profile   |
| GetRecommendationHistory | Historical interventions       |
| GetAssessmentTimeline    | Assessment chronology          |
| GetExplanation           | Explainability report          |
| GetKnowledgeModule       | Active ontology and rules      |
| GetCountyStatistics      | Population nutrition analytics |
| GetClinicalDashboard     | Administrative dashboard       |

Query Handlers optimize data retrieval while preserving domain consistency.

---

## 19.35.4 CQRS Workflow

```text
                Client Request
                     │
          ┌──────────┴──────────┐
          │                     │
      Command               Query
          │                     │
          ▼                     ▼
  Command Handler       Query Handler
          │                     │
          ▼                     ▼
 Application Services     Read Repository
          │                     │
          ▼                     ▼
 Domain Model          Read-Optimized DTOs
          │
          ▼
Repositories → Database
```

This separation improves readability, simplifies testing, and prepares the architecture for future scalability without prematurely introducing distributed complexity.

---

# 19.36 Dependency Injection Strategy

To reduce coupling and facilitate testing, dependencies are supplied through constructor injection rather than being instantiated directly within application services.

A typical Application Service depends only on interfaces, such as:

* `IAssessmentRepository`
* `IInferenceEngine`
* `IKnowledgeRepository`
* `IRecommendationRepository`
* `IExplanationRepository`
* `IEventPublisher`
* `IAuditService`

Concrete implementations are configured at application startup using a dependency injection container or equivalent wiring mechanism.

This approach enables the use of mock implementations during testing and supports future replacement of infrastructure components without modifying business logic.

---

# 19.37 Package Organization

The physical organization of the codebase mirrors the logical architecture.

```text
backend/
│
├── application/
│   ├── commands/
│   ├── queries/
│   ├── services/
│   ├── events/
│   └── handlers/
│
├── domain/
│   ├── assessment/
│   ├── knowledge/
│   ├── inference/
│   ├── intervention/
│   ├── monitoring/
│   ├── explainability/
│   └── shared/
│
├── infrastructure/
│   ├── persistence/
│   ├── repositories/
│   ├── prolog/
│   ├── ontology/
│   ├── knowledge_graph/
│   └── integrations/
│
├── interfaces/
│   ├── api/
│   ├── serializers/
│   └── authentication/
│
└── config/
```

This organization reinforces bounded context boundaries and facilitates independent evolution of application components.

---

# 19.38 Technology Mapping

| Architectural Concept    | Implementation Technology                 |
| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |
| Presentation Layer       | React + Vite                              |
| REST API                 | Django REST Framework                     |
| Authentication           | JWT (SimpleJWT)                           |
| Application Layer        | Python Application Services               |
| Domain Layer             | Python Dataclasses / Pydantic Models      |
| Repository Layer         | Django ORM Repositories                   |
| Knowledge Representation | SWI-Prolog                                |
| Ontology                 | OWL/RDF                                   |
| Knowledge Graph          | RDF Graph Database / NetworkX (prototype) |
| Symbolic Reasoning       | PySWIP                                    |
| Database                 | PostgreSQL                                |
| Cache                    | Redis                                     |
| Background Jobs          | Celery                                    |
| API Documentation        | OpenAPI / Swagger                         |
| Testing                  | Pytest + Django Test Framework            |
| Containerization         | Docker                                    |
| Deployment               | Nginx + Gunicorn + PostgreSQL             |

---

# 19.39 Architectural Governance

The long-term success of NutriLogic depends not only on its initial design but also on preserving architectural integrity as the platform evolves.

To achieve this, the project adopts the following governance principles:

1. All new functionality shall be assigned to an existing bounded context or justified through the creation of a new context.
2. Business rules shall remain within the Domain Layer and not migrate into controllers, serializers, or ORM models.
3. External integrations shall communicate through Ports and Adapters.
4. Clinical reasoning shall remain exclusively within the symbolic reasoning subsystem unless formally extended through the neuro-symbolic architecture.
5. All architectural changes shall be documented using Architecture Decision Records (ADRs).
6. Knowledge base modifications shall undergo clinical validation and version control before deployment.
7. Continuous Integration (CI) pipelines shall include architectural fitness tests to detect dependency violations and preserve layer boundaries.

---

# 19.40 Expanded Architecture Decision Record

## ADR-019: Adoption of Lightweight CQRS

### Status

Accepted

### Context

As NutriLogic grows, application services must manage increasingly complex workflows while also supporting efficient information retrieval. Combining read and write responsibilities within the same service classes reduces clarity and complicates maintenance.

### Decision

Adopt a lightweight Command–Query Responsibility Segregation (CQRS) pattern within the Application Layer. Separate command handling from query handling while retaining a shared domain model and single relational database.

### Rationale

* Improves separation of concerns.
* Simplifies testing of application workflows.
* Supports future scalability.
* Avoids unnecessary complexity associated with full CQRS and event sourcing.
* Aligns naturally with the event-driven architecture introduced earlier.

### Consequences

The Application Layer introduces distinct command and query handlers, increasing the number of implementation classes but resulting in clearer responsibilities and improved maintainability.

# 19.42 Chapter Summary

This chapter has established the **Domain-Driven Application Architecture** that serves as the structural backbone of NutriLogic. Through the adoption of Domain-Driven Design, Hexagonal Architecture, event-driven collaboration, lightweight CQRS, and explicit architectural governance, the application is organized around clinical capabilities rather than framework technologies.

The chapter first introduced the strategic domain model, identifying the principal bounded contexts responsible for nutrition assessment, knowledge management, symbolic inference, intervention planning, explainability, monitoring, and analytics. It then developed the tactical domain model, defining aggregate roots, entities, value objects, repositories, and domain services that preserve domain integrity and encapsulate clinical behaviour.

Building upon these foundations, the architecture incorporated ports and adapters, an Anti-Corruption Layer for integrating the SWI-Prolog reasoning engine, repository abstractions, dependency rules, and domain events to coordinate runtime workflows while maintaining loose coupling. Finally, the chapter presented an implementation strategy grounded in lightweight CQRS, dependency injection, technology mapping, and architectural governance to ensure that the system remains maintainable, extensible, and aligned with professional software engineering practices.

By treating symbolic reasoning, semantic knowledge, and explainability as first-class architectural concerns, this chapter transforms NutriLogic from a conventional web application into a **knowledge-centric, neuro-symbolic Clinical Nutrition Decision Support System (CNDSS)**. The resulting architecture provides a robust foundation upon which the remaining implementation chapters—Database Architecture, REST API Design, Frontend Architecture, Deployment, Testing, and Neuro-Symbolic Extensions—can be systematically developed.

---

## 19.43 Introduction

Architectural concepts are often presented independently, making it difficult to understand how the various components collaborate during system operation. To demonstrate the cohesion of the Domain-Driven Application Architecture, this section presents an end-to-end clinical scenario that traces a patient assessment through every major architectural component of NutriLogic.

The case study illustrates how bounded contexts, application services, repositories, domain events, the Anti-Corruption Layer, the symbolic reasoning engine, and the explainability subsystem cooperate to produce clinically governed nutrition recommendations. By revisiting the same patient throughout the chapter, the architecture is grounded in a realistic workflow that reflects the Nutrition Care Process (NCP) and the Ministry of Health Kenya Nutrient Profile Model.

---

# 19.44 Running Clinical Scenario

## 19.44.1 Patient Profile

The following hypothetical patient is used consistently throughout this chapter.

| Attribute          | Value                                          |
| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- |
| Patient Identifier | P-2026-0001                                    |
| Name               | Jane Achieng' *(fictional)*                    |
| Age                | 46 years                                       |
| Sex                | Female                                         |
| County             | Kisumu                                         |
| Height             | 1.63 m                                         |
| Weight             | 84.5 kg                                        |
| BMI                | 31.8 kg/m² (Obesity Class I)                   |
| Blood Pressure     | 148/94 mmHg                                    |
| Physical Activity  | Low                                            |
| Dietary Pattern    | High sodium, low fruit and vegetable intake    |
| Medical History    | Hypertension                                   |
| Primary Goal       | Weight reduction and blood pressure management |

This fictional patient serves solely as an architectural example and does not represent any real individual.

---

# 19.45 End-to-End Architectural Walkthrough

## Step 1 – User Authentication

Jane authenticates through the React frontend using JSON Web Tokens (JWT). The request is handled by the Users & Profiles bounded context, which validates credentials and retrieves her longitudinal health profile.

**Architectural Components:**

* React SPA
* Authentication API
* User Application Service
* User Repository
* PostgreSQL

**Domain Event:**

```
UserAuthenticated
```

---

## Step 2 – Nutrition Assessment

Jane completes a comprehensive nutrition assessment, including anthropometric measurements, dietary intake, lifestyle information, and clinical observations.

The Assessment Application Service validates the submitted information using domain entities and value objects before persisting the assessment.

**Aggregate Root:**

```
NutritionAssessment
```

**Value Objects:**

* BMI
* BloodPressure
* PhysicalActivityLevel
* DietaryDiversityScore

**Domain Event:**

```
AssessmentSubmitted
```

---

## Step 3 – Event Publication

Following successful validation, the Assessment bounded context publishes an immutable domain event.

```
AssessmentSubmitted
```

Subscribers include:

* Inference Context
* Monitoring Context
* Analytics Context

Importantly, the Assessment context has no direct knowledge of its subscribers, preserving loose coupling.

---

## Step 4 – Clinical Inference

The Inference Application Service receives the event and retrieves the assessment through the Assessment Repository interface.

Before invoking SWI-Prolog, the Anti-Corruption Layer translates the object-oriented assessment into symbolic facts.

Example translation:

```prolog
patient(p_2026_0001).
age(p_2026_0001,46).
sex(p_2026_0001,female).
county(p_2026_0001,kisumu).
bmi(p_2026_0001,31.8).
blood_pressure(p_2026_0001,148,94).
condition(p_2026_0001,hypertension).
physical_activity(p_2026_0001,low).
dietary_pattern(p_2026_0001,high_sodium).
```

The symbolic reasoning engine evaluates these facts against the modular knowledge base.

---

## Step 5 – Symbolic Reasoning

The Prolog engine performs forward and backward chaining to determine:

* Nutrition diagnosis
* Dietary interventions
* Therapeutic meal planning
* Behavioural recommendations
* Referral requirements

During inference, every activated rule is recorded.

Example:

```
Rule KB-HTN-021 activated
Reason:
BMI > 30
Hypertension present
High sodium intake
Low physical activity
```

This trace is forwarded to the Explainability Context.

---

## Step 6 – Recommendation Generation

Inference results are translated back into domain entities.

The Recommendation Aggregate produces a structured intervention plan.

Example recommendations include:

* Reduce sodium intake.
* Adopt DASH-inspired meal patterns using locally available foods.
* Increase consumption of legumes, fruits, and vegetables.
* Engage in at least 150 minutes of moderate physical activity weekly.
* Schedule follow-up blood pressure assessment.

Each recommendation includes:

* Clinical priority
* Guideline reference
* Rule trace
* Supporting evidence
* Contraindications (if any)

---

## Step 7 – Explainability

The Explainability Context converts symbolic reasoning traces into clinician- and patient-friendly narratives.

Example:

> *This recommendation was generated because your Body Mass Index indicates obesity, your recorded blood pressure meets the criteria for hypertension, and your dietary assessment indicates excessive sodium consumption. These findings satisfy Rule KB-HTN-021 within the Ministry of Health Kenya Nutrient Profile Model knowledge base.*

Every recommendation therefore remains transparent, auditable, and clinically justified.

---

## Step 8 – Monitoring

The Monitoring Context records:

* Initial measurements
* Behavioural goals
* Follow-up schedule
* Intervention adherence

A follow-up assessment is automatically scheduled.

Domain Event:

```
FollowUpScheduled
```

---

## Step 9 – Analytics

Finally, anonymized data are forwarded to the Analytics Context.

Population-level analytics may include:

* prevalence of hypertension,
* obesity trends,
* county-level dietary patterns,
* intervention effectiveness,
* adherence statistics.

No personally identifiable information is exposed within analytical workflows.

---
